# Character Bible — *The Hour of Ash* (run 2)

> **Purpose.** The canonical home for the cast's VOICE and DISTINCTNESS, with one entry per NAMED
> character. This is a **living document**. The Architect seeded it in voice mode (2026-10-18), and the
> **Writer adds an entry the moment a new named character first appears on the page, before
> finalizing that chapter.** Dialogue Polish writes from these cards. The Evaluator and the Continuity
> Guardian audit against them. Facts and timeline live in `ENTITY_STATE.yaml`; the narration's voice and
> the anti-pattern budget live in `voice-dna.md`. This file is the per-character voice layer.
>
> **Sample lines are illustrations, not script.** Never paste one into the manuscript. The n-gram gates
> will flag it, and a line that appears here and on the page has become a catchphrase.

## How distinctness works here (read before adding a character)

Distinctness is **NOT** "give everyone a verbal tic." Characters are separated on **four axes**, in this
order of power:

1. **Attention / worldview:** what they NOTICE and reduce the world to. The deepest differentiator.
2. **Syntax / register:** sentence shape, clause habits, formality, questions against statements.
3. **Evasion:** what they will NOT say.
4. **Physicality / silence:** the body or the pause that stands in for speech.

There is no fifth thing. **Nobody has a verbal tic.** Each card also carries a **bleed guard**: what this
character must never borrow, usually from the narrator. When a narrator's habits leak onto the cast,
everyone starts to sound alike.

**Language on the page.** All speech is rendered in English, and the register signals the language.
Ancient speakers use no modern idiom and no modern units. Only Fenn, and Mara when she speaks English,
use modern idiom. See `voice-dna.md` §1, Dialogue conventions.

## Card format

```
#### [CHARACTER] — [role] (chapters)
- Attention / worldview:
- Vocabulary band:
- Syntax fingerprint:
- Rhythm:
- Evasion — never says:
- Physicality / silence:
- Verbal tic: none — distinguished by <axis>
- Bleed guard:
- Chaos on the page (subjects, never phrases):
- DISTINCTNESS GUARANTEE:
- Sample line:
```

---

## PRINCIPALS

#### MARA REYES — narrator and protagonist; objects conservator, Philadelphia, b. 1965 (Ch.1–44)
- **Attention / worldview:** what a thing is made of, how it was joined, where it will fail and how it
  was mended. She reads people the same way: their hands, their teeth, the repaired places in them. She
  trusts what stays put (made things, the dead) and keeps a bag packed against what moves. **Only Mara
  notices materials and joins.**
- **Vocabulary band:** educated American, late-1990s frame of reference (answering machine, Pentax,
  Discman, payphones, SEPTA, Rite Aid, film sent away to develop), with nothing after spring 1998. Lab
  nouns leak in sparingly (squeeze-out, consolidant, chain lines, cold join, birch tar), never as a
  report form. She reads Attic Greek and Latin from books. Her spoken Greek begins as a classroom
  reconstruction that 1st-century ears find comic.
- **Syntax fingerprint:** **Narration:** long, clause-stacking sentences when interested, linked by
  semicolons and "and," with one sideways clause that comes back; plain short declaratives when afraid.
  **Speech:** quick and qualified, often answering a question with a question. In early Greek (Ch.4–10)
  her lines are short, careful and wrong. In Latin (Part Two) she is competent and accented. In English
  with Fenn she is fast, contracted and 1998. **As written in Ch.6 (synced 2026-10-19):** her Greek carries no
  contractions at all ("I shall not need to come down", "Had I any silver, you would have had silver", "Latin
  is not learned on this road"), so contractions mark her English ("They're fine."; the cliff speech; the
  plea to the rock). Her first Greek aloud in a month is to Valens in the lane.
- **Rhythm:** set chapter by chapter by the ledger's gear. Fragments are a pressure signal, so she
  rarely uses them in calm narration.
- **Evasion — never says:** the word *love* in her own narration until Part Two (first use no earlier
  than Ch.19, and rare after that); her father's death stated plainly (she circles it through his
  objects); any claim to be brave; any request to be looked after. After Ch.25 she will not put
  **Fenn's date** in the future tense (no "it will open in seventy-nine") until Ch.36. Hope for an
  earlier opening (Ch.30) and the argument over whether the door would take the children (Ch.33) are
  allowed; what she refuses is the date. She never says "Margaret" to Fenn.
- **Physicality / silence:** steady, competent hands that betray her only at thresholds. She **touches
  surfaces to read them** (a thumbnail along a seam, a fingertip on a glaze, her palm on painted
  plaster). When she cannot speak she *inspects* something. Under pressure her hands act neatly by
  themselves (sample-05).
- **Verbal tic:** none — distinguished by attention (materials and joins) and syntax (spiral in
  interest, clip in fear)
- **Bleed guard:** her dry asides and her eye for materials must never migrate into anyone else's
  dialogue. Avoid "the conservator in me" as a recurring phrase (at-risk list).
- **Chaos on the page (subjects, never phrases):** **teeth** (full surfacings Ch.7, 20, 31; glancing
  only in Ch.2 floss, Ch.5 Kallia inspects hers, Ch.15 Valens's, Ch.24 the midwife's; the Ch.24
  midwife is the book's only introduction-by-teeth, and the rule stays at most one in any four chapters).
  **All-or-nothing** (Ch.1, 5, 7, 9, 20, 29, 33; the ending must read as accepting a seam, not as this
  distortion). **Mrs. Dunphy** (Ch.7 green cardigan; Ch.24 brown; Ch.41 unsure it was long division).
  **Mundane breaks** (Ch.6 the scream, Ch.20 the pebble, Ch.22 the sneeze).
- **CONTINUITY COMMITMENT (from late Ch.7, ¶378; logged 2026-10-19 from the Ch.7 re-evaluation):** "from the
  next night I kept even my own company in Greek." From that night on, Mara does **not speak English to
  herself**: no muttered English, no English said aloud alone in the dark, no English prayer or curse under
  her breath, and no English address or name recited to keep it. **English to a listener does not break it:**
  her English with Fenn (Ch.25 on) is speech to another person. Any later solitary English must be written as
  a **deliberate, visible break** of this commitment, and the page should register it as one. (Her English
  *thoughts* are the narration itself and are not affected.) The commitment is her all-or-nothing distortion
  escalating inside one chapter (¶148 public silence → ¶378 private English); the beat of renouncing a register
  is SPENT after these two (see at-risk after Ch.7, cycle 1).
- **Ch.8 as written (Writer, 2026-10-19; Parnassus, midwinter AD 71, a few days after the turn of the year. SYNCED to the
  final page 2026-10-19, after dialogue-polish, disruptor, continuity repair and polish cycle 1; every quote below is on the
  page, checked by script):** the commitment holds: she speaks no English at all, aloud or to herself, in the whole chapter
  (the cave included: she says nothing to the rock). Her Greek stays uncontracted ("I talk," / "Not well." / "What has
  stopped?" / "Why not wrap them over, as we have?" / "It is bread." / "Let me have it," / "Wet leather keeps whatever shape
  it dries in," / "Longer than you will like." / "There is no he." / "It is very cold."). "I left the flashlight at the
  bottom of my bag under the stair." (the page gives no reason; the reason is the reader's since Ch.5). At the crack she
  "sent my hand in to the wrist", keeps her cheek on the slab, and "I left my hand in there until I had been fair to it";
  no scream and no blow, only "There was nobody to laugh." (standing alone since cycle 1); she tucks the hand "inside the
  himation, under my arm, where it ached as it warmed". She finds the pale scratches her shovel left in November, "after
  the last of the earth was out". Her repairer's eye works on a fawnskin "tawed with the fat still on the belly", the
  stranger's strap "patched over the instep with a square of paler leather, sewn on with a thong and set on askew", and a
  shrunk sandal she works flat with Timo's hair oil (useful, not a credential). Her joke, spoken (she cannot sing),
  finishes the husband verse: "And he was so small," … "that I lost him on the boat." / "Smaller," … "I have made him
  bigger for the song." "I was laughing too." It is the first laugh a joke of hers starts, and the first she shares with a
  group (Ch.4 ¶73 she laughed with the goat-boy "without knowing what at"; Ch.7's step laugh was silent and alone).
  Knowledge asides by hand: 2 (Euripides for the cry; the Prometheus aside on the fennel pith). The frozen cloaks are no
  longer hers: they are Nikaso's story on the stair (Plutarch, *De primo frigido* 953D, behind it, unnamed). Unprompted
  memory: the Vermont brothers' Christmas-tree lot paragraph was CUT (AUTHOR-CP2-2, 2026-10-19; a stand-alone detour).
  The chapter's one 1998 memory is now her pay, which "turned up in a bank downtown every other Friday" (no intersection on
  the page), inside the walk-back paragraph.
- **Ch.9 as written (Writer, 2026-10-19; Delphi, late winter AD 71. SYNCED to the final page 2026-10-19, after
  dialogue-polish, disruptor, continuity repair, polish cycle 1 and the bending fix with finalize lines F1–F4; every quote below
  is on the page, checked by script):** the commitment holds: no English anywhere, aloud or alone. **The decision to consult is
  shown, not told to Kallia:** she climbs the Sacred Way "to find a priest" and composes the question in Greek on the gymnasium
  lane ("*Where is the way back?*" too short; "*Where is the door I came in by, now it is shut?*" nearer): "I tried it over in a
  murmur, and it sounded like somebody patting her pockets for a key. Whether I believed in Apollo was beside the point. He kept
  the one window in Phocis that took questions about doors, and the cave had been shut to me twice." Public identity: the slave
  calls her "Kallia's Syracusan"; to "Where does a woman learn bronze?": "In Syracuse," … "which was where I was from, now."
  Her Greek stays uncontracted ("So that nobody can say she bought herself." / "And if he sells her before then? To some man in
  Amphissa who has never—", cut off by the cutter, the chapter's one em-dash / "If you heat it," … "the bowl will come off." /
  "That it runs long before bronze does," / "No forge," / "The rod takes the weight. The wax is only for the eye." / "It will
  come apart along the same line," … "and in no new place, and the next mender will have nothing to undo but wax." / "It is not a
  smith's work. That is why it will last." / "Tell them it is for the god." / "It holds. It is the seam I am taking out." / "I
  can join it again." / "With this in it? Years. If nobody knocks it over." / "I have nothing until he pays me." / "It is a
  curse." / "Onto it. From above." / "The man, I suppose."). To Kallia the god's price is one line, not a list: "He says a stater
  for the god," … "and a goat that shakes, and more after that."
  **Craft, as hands at work (no report form):** spots the old lead solder ringing the bowl and the gas cavities at the break;
  refuses the forge; rakes the burnt clay core out with a bent nail, files the smith's rod to fit ("It was too fat, and I filed
  it, and it was still too fat.", the chapter's ugly sentence), seats the halves on it; fills with the bee-man's wax and lamp
  soot, then with Valens's pitch melted in. **The fill comes out three times, on uneven rungs:** the first dig-out is implied
  ("On the second try"), after the wax "shone" as "a thin gold thread" in low sun; the second is the bee-man's "Out it comes,"
  after a thumbprint, "the whorls raised in the fill"; the third follows the line seen sighting along the sun on a bronze "skin
  like an orange's", "and my fill was smooth", and goes "too fast": the knife "laid my thumb open at the base. I sucked it until
  it stopped." The fourth fill is pricked with bristles: "I had lost it myself." **The dawn try is lost to the house:** "Kallia
  wanted the jars filled before she lit her fire, and I was at the lions with the first of them; the stand would have to go to
  the priest as it stood." **All-or-nothing as a wager:** "If you find it, pay me nothing. If you cannot, pay me the smith's
  price." **Wrong reaction at the peak:** the priest drops the coins on her palm and wipes his fingers; "He had not touched it.
  Perhaps it was the soot." / "Thank you," … "and bent my head to him as well." **Body rebel (the book's first of its kind):** in
  Kallia's basin "the cut in my thumb came open in the water and put a thread of red into the black. I cleaned round it. It went
  on coloring the basin for a while, fainter each time my hands went in, and then the water stayed black." Memory/noise: the red
  crayon on a schoolroom radiator (a smell inside a sentence, no person); the hardware store off Fortieth Street, FOLDED
  (AUTHOR-CP2-2, 2026-10-19) into the rod sentence as one clause that comes straight back to the smith: "A hardware store off
  Fortieth Street sold pins like it singly out of little wooden drawers, with a cat asleep on the counter scale; the smith had
  sent his second-best file with this one…" (no lost name any more). Money: the priest's coins knotted "into a rag with the two brass ones from 1998, the poet and the
  other"; she owes Valens an obol and is holding it ("I had his obol in the rag. I kept my place."). At the notice (pressure
  level 1): asides die; "I read it through again. The rag had gone damp in my fist." / "I was looking at him. I looked at his
  face." Knowledge asides by credential: 0. Teeth: none.
- **Ch.10 as written (Writer, 2026-10-19; one afternoon in Kallia's court. SYNCED to the FINAL page 2026-10-19, after
  dialogue-polish, hook-craft, the disruptor, continuity repair, polish cycle 1 and the PATH fix; every quote below is on the page,
  checked by script):** the commitment holds: no English anywhere. **She reads Ariston's letter aloud, whole and unedited.** Her
  reading voice is the instrument, and the page no longer explains it: the eyes-a-line-ahead paragraph and the written-vs-spoken
  Greek paragraph were cut by the disruptor, and the speech-ledger sentence by polish cycle 1. The perfection is shown through
  Kallia instead, four lines after she corrects the length of his absence: "Kallia corrected none of it. The Greek was his, from
  beginning to end." Her own Greek stays uncontracted and short ("Her father is called Iamblichos. It is a name from that
  country." / "It does not say. He says she keeps his house well." (now faithful: the letter has "and keeps my house well") /
  "It runs off, I suppose. They have less of it there." / "It says only a man." / "Yes." / "He says the arm has knit," (her wrong
  comfort, reaching for the wound she can see) / "How old was he, when he went?" / "I can write, as well as read. If you want to
  send him word, when the boats go east." / "Papyrus. The priest's household would sell me a sheet." / "Iamblichos." / "Somebody
  going east would take it for a cup. As the Elean did."). **Cognitive distortion in action:** an all-or-nothing rule about
  letters that the afternoon disproves ("Whatever was in a letter, I thought, a person read it at once, standing at the door it
  came to, and the pots could wait."; Kallia has just "returned to the pots") and the misread yawn ("She had been on her feet
  since the dark, and the Elean had come at the worst of the day; I read the next part faster."). **Irrelevant thought
  (present-scene, no memory):** the Van Pelt bookmark postcard was CUT (AUTHOR-CP2-2, 2026-10-19). Homs stays as a fact she
  places on the map while she reads, and the letter's words pass unheard: "Emesa was Homs, on the Orontes, and the mountains
  with snow on them would be the Lebanon. I had river and range laid out as they lay on the map, with the road running west
  from the town through a gap in the hills to the sea, and the oxen and the man to drive them went by while I was still on
  that road. Kallia had not left the roof." Present-scene history (not foreshadowing): "Jerusalem had fallen in the summer just gone, and its
  temple with it, burning. I knew it the way I knew the Armada, as a date on a page with a fire in it." After
  Nikaso, one sentence (no anaphora, no "could have"): "Nobody else in that court or in that lane could read a word, and I might
  as easily have made the roof into so much talk about rain and left out the father with no son of his own." Hands on the
  letter: "The clay broke from the knot at the first push, and the thread slid off over the end. Unfolded, the papyrus was mostly
  creases and needed both my hands to keep it flat."; she "showed her the opening word with a fingertip". At the close: "I read
  it all to myself, from the greeting down to the date, and rolled it up." (no refold, no size or weight) and "I put the
  letter in behind the cumin." No body break of her own (Kallia's yawn carries it). Ends on the afternoon going on: "Half the
  basket was still to clean, and I pulled it back onto my knees." … "The step was in shade. It was a good while yet till the
  lamps." No teeth, no credential asides, no laugh, "could have" 0, no parallel drawn between Ariston's choice and hers, in
  narration or anywhere.
- **Ch.11 as written (Writer, 2026-10-20; "Valens's Yard", the Kirrha road below Delphi, late winter AD 71, about a week. SYNCED
  to the FINAL page 2026-10-20, after dialogue-polish, hook-craft, the disruptor, continuity repair, polish cycle 1 and finalize
  lines F1, F3, F4; every quote below is on the page, checked by script):** the commitment holds: no English anywhere. Her Greek
  stays uncontracted ("I have three," / "Kallia said you would stand for her house." / "Two now," … "and two more after the
  harvest." / "I need somebody," … "to carry my question in to the god." / "You have not heard what I would pay." / "They will be
  looking at the god. Not at the man with the goat." / "There is the board in the square, too." / "Then sell me back the watch," /
  "Have you let it stop?" / "It is fast," / "That will break on the first steep road," / "I have not asked to be paid." / "It
  lasted you to the olives." / "I know. I have had my feet on it all day." / "It is mine," … "He is keeping it for me." / "These
  have been too long in the sun," / "A fig ought to give when you press it." / "In my country they split them and press them flat,
  in rounds, with a leaf on top." / "What has turning them to do with wasps?" / "Somebody else would have to open mine," (the
  leaving never said; dialogue-polish cut the draft's spoken version) / "Once they are old enough to be late." / "For whatever they
  are going to. Lessons. Work. Each other." / "So that two people can be in the same place at the same time, and neither of them
  stands there waiting." / "The rich," … "And people who work all night, and sleep in the day." / "A lock does. In my country every
  door has its own." / "She is a mule." / to Kallia: "Mine. I will carry it." / "No." (her one bare "No.") / "I have less than three
  now, Praxias," … "And the god's share comes out of that first." / "You had your back to him the whole time he was in the gate." /
  "Perhaps his olives are uphill." / "Where would you stand, in all that?" / "You have been in yourself, surely. Or sent a question
  in by somebody else." / "Suppose he has nothing for me." / "That is between me and the god." / "You read it." / "You said you heard
  the board read out." / "Did he take you to the priests," … "and have it cut on the wall?" (one qualified question; the stacked pair
  was Felix's shape) / "Before whom?" / "There is no paper." / "Who is to say he is free, then?" / "And if somebody takes him?" /
  "Who took yours?" / "Hold this end," … "Hard." / "Nobody. I cannot meet Praxias's price." / "At the door. Inside, though?" / "The
  priest will write your name down. You told me so yourself." / "The clerk wrote Maira." / "Mara." / "How does a goat get from Kirrha
  up to Delphi?"). **Her origin is no longer cut at a dash:** she finishes "In my country every door has its own." and he leaves on
  it. **She pays Valens's obol** first, before she asks anything ("I untied the rag first and held out the obol." / "For the pitch,";
  the Ch.9 debt is CLOSED). Her money is "three drachmas in the rag at my belt, all that the priest had paid me" (ENTITY CH11-2
  fixed: the rag, not a himation knot). **Her name is given:** "Mara." (Valens has not said it aloud); the priest gets "The
  clerk's." **Craft as hands at work, no report form:** the breeching's old thread "had been sawn through, a little at every downhill
  step, by the grit in the stitch holes" (F4 cut the "not broken so much as" correction), and the turn is re-sewn "with a needle at
  each end of the thread, so that every hole was filled from both sides", in thread pitched on Valens's pot with the blunt awl "as it
  was"; a girth; a headstall "sewn on by somebody who had never seen one sewn" (the absent-workman verdict, 1); a packsaddle pad
  emptied and restuffed by Felix; the breastband's matted felt cut away and "a new one of fleece, wool side in". **Cognitive
  distortion in action (all-or-nothing):** "If Praxias would not do it for three, nobody in Delphi would do it for three. Kallia had
  one cousin, and I had just spent him." It sends her along the wall to the yard. **Irrelevant thought (present scene, mid-pricing):**
  the quail "had the expression of a toll collector at the end of a shift". **Failed management (stopped work):** "I had the headstall
  over my knees, and I put no stitch in it." (the cast's ONE work-suspended tell in Ch.11 after F1; see at-risk). **The near-kiss
  from her side:** "and lifted my head, and his face was there." / "The lamp was on its nail behind me; it lit him and left me in the
  dark." / "He came nearer, and my face lifted to meet his." **Relief at the refused kiss (the emotional surprise), through the seat
  and appetite, no knees, no simile (hands and voice rested):** "I sat down. The packsaddle had been on its trestle at my back, and I
  was on it, with the cut ends of the thread still pinched in my hand. Everything in me that had been held up came down, and I was
  hungry. I wanted bread, and whatever Kallia had in the pot, and I wanted it now." **Her question rewritten** on the back of the
  sherd from *The door that let me in is shut. Where is another?* to *Where is my road home?* after Valens's "Ask for a road." **She
  keeps his secret** in front of Felix ("I kept quiet, and Felix brought his questions to me instead."). Waiting, bare: "I waited for
  him to tell me why." / "Why?" I said. Memories: none (present thought only: "standing in a kitchen in Philadelphia with a tap in
  it"). Kallia's obol for the papyrus is still unspent and "knotted in with the remains of the priest's money". Credential asides 0;
  teeth 0; laughs 0; English 0; could-have 0. WATCH: "Who…?" is her most-used question opener in Ch.11 (Who read it out / Who sewed
  it last / Who is to say / Who took yours / Who told you); give her other forms in Ch.12.
- **Ch.12 as written (Writer, 2026-10-20; "The Kirrha Road", In medias res, the road up from Kirrha, early spring AD 71, three days
  before the god's day. SYNCED to the FINAL page 2026-10-20 (polish cycle 1 + F1–F2; continuity W-22): every quote below is on the
  page, checked by script):** the commitment holds: no English anywhere. **She asks NO question in the whole chapter** (the subtext
  law: she answers his telling by not asking; every "?" on the page belongs to someone else). Her Greek stays uncontracted ("Somebody
  will come by," / "They will think it was robbers." / "Only the rim is bent." / "I read it twice," / "My question wanted carrying." /
  "Praxias wanted half a drachma over three." / "It is only the wool," / "It was my father's," / "It is more than the god wants," /
  "She is full," / "She is the god's," / "I heard it." / "I was watching something else." / "You need not take it in," … "There are
  other days. Next month would do as well." / "They will be there. Those two, or others." / "Kallia will hear at the spring tomorrow
  that a man is dead below the ford," / "I am not telling Kallia a story. She gets the whole of it from me, or nothing."). **Pressure
  level 2 in the fight (the rehearsal):** asides die; plain verbs; "Wine. Sweat."; untagged "Mara." / "From the ditch."; *Rudiarius*
  unglossed; sense narrowed to sound ("The ditch hid them. I heard them.") and then to sight for the cap. **The grab (no hand named;
  she feels the wool, not the man):** "My himation came tight across my throat from behind. I was pulled back against somebody." **The
  camera swung in panic:** "The strap was round my neck under my clothes. My hand was on it. I dragged at it, and the camera came clear
  of my clothes, and I twisted round in his grip and swung it at his head." / "It caught him above the ear. I felt it in my wrist."
  **The anchor:** "The cap was off. It went rolling along the road on its rim, small and black, wobbling and righting itself over the
  ruts with the white letters on its face going under and coming up again, … until it met the edge of the oil and tipped over in it,
  face up." She leaves it: "I went past where the cap lay in the oil, its letters dark with it, and kept going." **Wrong reaction (the
  strap wound neat, a new act):** "I wound the strap round the camera. I wound until there was no strap left" / "The camera was still
  in my arms." **The steadying (the emotional surprise, his not hers):** "I took his wrists, one in each hand, and pressed them flat to
  the stone beside the fall" / "Under my right palm his old scar was a raised line, and I could feel in it the marks where each stitch
  had gone in." (her mender's eye at the peak; the Ch.6 ladder NOT re-quoted) / "and I did not speak." / "It stopped by degrees."
  **Asides return in order:** materials first (the camera: hair "wound into the little crank on top"; "The ring at the front of the
  lens had taken the blow and was pushed in at one point. In the finder the plane trees stood upright and in focus."; the cloak: "It
  was good wool, fulled hard, darned at the hem with green thread."; "Cold water. Hot would have set it."), then the Calf-Bearer ("if
  the Calf-Bearer had been twelve and in a temper"), then Orestes ("Apollo had been taking money from men with blood on them since
  Orestes, and had never been known to send any back."). **Why the Pentax is with her:** the lodgers in rows along Kallia's gallery;
  "The camera was the thing in that bag a thief would take first, and the thing I could least explain to anybody who took it"; then
  "Its cap was still in the oil by the ditch, where I meant it to stay." (her choice, left unexplained; the flashlight is not
  mentioned). **The Pentax after Ch.12:** no lens cap (LOST at the ditch on the Kirrha road); the front ring of the lens pushed in at
  one point (dented; the page does not say whether a filter would fit, and nobody tests it); the finder in focus; it rides outside her
  clothes once ("where I had forgotten it") and then "went back inside my clothes". Any later scene with the camera has an uncapped
  lens and never mentions a cap (see voice-dna §6, the sample-13 note). **Money (CH11-3 CLOSED):** she paid the goat herself ("and the
  rag was thin after it"); of the dead man's silver: "What the goat had left in my rag would not have bought the god his cake. This
  would, with plenty over." Praxias's fee is NOT named on the page; it comes out of the "plenty over", implied. She tipped the silver
  into the rag ("with the little that was mine"), and the empty purse was let go in the stream. Her rag now holds the priest's
  remainder, Kallia's papyrus obol, the two 1998 coins and a dead man's denarii (never named as a sum, never itemized again).
  **Knowledge:** she knows the whole frame now (rudis, oath, Celer, the dowry share, the tablet, the record office, the freedman, the
  notice's timing) and has his leave to tell Kallia: "Kallia, then. Nobody else." Body: "The skin was raised in a line across the front
  of my throat where the edge of the wool had dragged, and it stung."; her left sandal thong re-knotted "double". Credential asides 0;
  teeth 0; laughs 0; memories 0 (as briefed); "could have" 0 in her narration.
- **Ch.13 as written (Writer, 2026-10-20; "The Seventh of Bysios", Parallel outside/inside, Delphi, the year's first consultation day,

  commitment holds: no English anywhere. **She asks again** (Ch.12's no-question shape is spent): her Greek stays uncontracted ("He did not
  say." / "If she does?" / "For the shoulders," … "Take them with water, and inside an hour you could turn your head without your hips." /
  "Have you seen them?" / "And the boy?" / "He puts his nose that close to everything," … "He could not find the join on his own
  grandfather's incense stand with a lamp held to it." / "Know yourself," … "And another. Nothing too much." / "Darker with what?" / "The
  stone," … "Rounded on top, about so high, carved over with a kind of net. It must be in there." / "Where was the woman?" / "Try." /
  "Surely the priest heard it." / "A road." / "The tall one. With the bruise on his face." / "He is carrying for me." / "The lead has held
  it this long," … "Another year will do it no harm." (and in narration "Before the bowl's turn came I meant to be gone.") / "Blood goes
  brown when it dries," / "It was heavy," / "Is it bad?" / "Has one died?" / "Did the priest give you anything about when?" … "A month, a
  year. Anything." / "West of what? West of here? Everything is west of here." / "Very far west. Farther than any of this." / "In fields," …
  "With fences round them, and the grass kept short, and a stone with the name on it for each." / "There are no walls." / "Where a board by
  the road says it does," / the tirade: "There is a mountain with vines on it beside every sea the Romans have ever reached. It is not an
  address. It is a description of the empire." / "It is a riddle," … "A riddle is nothing. I might walk ten years with it and find a plaster
  door every other mile." / "Tomorrow." / "I want to see it shut for myself." / "Only the mountain part," … "Say just that piece again, from
  its beginning." / "You cannot start it in the middle," / "Do not," … "It is in my head." / "Why his name?" … "At the table." / "Yours?" /
  "Who is Theano?" / "Which is worse for them," … "the steps down or the steps up?" / "For the knees. They take the ache down, for half a
  day. Swallow them whole, with water, and do not chew them." / "Why give them any?" / "He is careful where he stands," / "My carrier heard
  a word in it that was not Greek. Twice, he told me. You gave it twice." / "You sent for me," … "Why?" / "When?" … "On which day is it
  open? The road." / "I can pay. There is silver." / "Was there a day in it? Was there one, and they cut it?" / "Which city?"). **Kallia
  given the whole story** at the lions before light, in order, summarized in one paragraph (no re-telling of the frame in Valens's words):
  "I left out his hands, which were his." The camera is "the thing of my father's that I carried inside my chiton, which I called only that,
  a heavy thing from my bag" (no lens, no cap). **Praxias paid** "out of the rag, in coins from the ford, into his hand" (no sum, never
  itemized). **The ibuprofen:** "I had the Advil in my belt, down to its last two." Offered to Valens below the altar, hidden under her
  himation; "He did not touch the two small white things in my palm." ("After," … "I want my head, in there."); "I put them back in the
  bottle." In the afternoon she "held them up to her where she sat on her husband's stone" and Theano takes both: the Advil is used up in
  Ch.13 and never mentioned after. **Cognitive distortion (all-or-nothing, in action):** on the walk down she decides from his silence that
  the god said there is no road ("By the lions I had it settled. I was almost calm."), then calls the real answer nothing ("A riddle is
  nothing."). **Irrelevant thought:** the board of clay ears ("They were all left ears."), broken off by Felix's vault. **One memory only
  (triggered, and it runs back into speech):** her father's carousel slide of the Tarquinia tomb, "thrown six feet wide in our kitchen one
  September night", with "a bedsheet tacked across the china for a screen", "a false door on the plaster in dark red, shut, and a mourner at
  each side of it"; clicked on to Volterra "while my question about what lay behind it was half out", the projector fan "breathing out its
  smell of hot dust in the dark room". It runs straight into her spoken tirade and does not close on a lost name. **No airport-gate
  memory:** it was cut and is not on the page. **Imagined first hour home** (present-scene hope, not memory): the heat off, "the vent in the
  hall in my socks", the freezer, "standing, with a fork". **Body rebel, new channel (skin), then an irrelevance at the peak, unexplained:**
  "The hair had stood up along both my arms under the wool, to the shoulder, and it would not lie down again. Over the woodstack the wall
  was bare but for a lizard, flat in the sun with its toes splayed." "It scanned." follows unannounced (the measure of Homer; the tail "like
  a suitcase roped onto the roof of a car that was already full"). Hands, voice and seat stay rested. **Her history knowledge:** "Except
  that Etna stood above the eastern sea, the one Syracuse looked out on, and the god, in the priest's verse, had said west."; Etruria;
  Pindar's iron chair, unnamed ("A poet had sat in it to sing for the god, five hundred years ago" … "and the poet had been a Boeotian.").
  She does NOT think of Vesuvius or Campania before the word: her list is "Or Sicily after all, or Massalia, or Spain."; the Bay of Naples
  and Campania: 0 (C-01). No reaction to "Pompeii." on the page (Ch.14). Credential asides 0; teeth 0; laughs 0; English 0; "could have" in

  tells no one** what the word means: to Valens's "She kept you." at the corner she says "Theano's jar was empty." (true: "She had me carry

  the dark, and let him think the god thought better of it."); to Kallia at the drain "She wanted somebody to hear about her stool," (true).
  At supper "Kallia asked me nothing."; Kallia's one question at midnight ("What did Theano want with you?") pays that off with no gloss
  (the draft's call-out pointer is cut, continuity W-08). **Her knowledge of AD 79, as present thought (voice-dna §0.3):** "I knew the
  year." It comes "before her mouth had quite closed on the name" … "Seventy-nine, late in the summer, in August."; the god's verse decoded
  ("The mountain with the vines on it was that mountain. Its sea to the west was the bay under it." and the dead along the roads outside the
  gates); the town from a plan ("I had the whole town from above, the way a plan in a book had shown it to me."; a lopsided oval on old
  lava, the forum at the west end, one straight street east; Fiorelli unnamed: "An Italian in the 1850s had cut the place into regions and
  blocks and doors and numbered every one, like sherds in a tray."; the House of the Faun, the House of the Tragic Poet; "What anybody in
  that town called a single street was beyond me."); the tombs (the bench row beyond the gate toward Herculaneum, more along the road toward
  Nuceria; she tries "to call up a doorway done in paint on any of them, and got only the benches."); Pliny the Younger unnamed ("The day
  was in two letters, as much of it as one man set down for Tacitus nearly thirty years afterward.", put into English "for a Latin
  examination"; "The mountain opened at midday." / "Stones fell on the far shore all that afternoon and all night." / "Roofs went in under
  them." / "In the morning came the thing the letters had no good word for." / "He was a boy of nine now, somewhere in the north of Italy,
  at his lessons."). **Cognitive distortion (catastrophizing, in action):** "A door that opened when it pleased could open on any day in
  eight years. This summer would do. For all anybody could tell me, it stood open tonight, on a road of tombs in the dark, with nothing
  going past it but a dog." **No outcome of her own is narrated;** intentions only ("I meant to go up as soon as it was light"). **The one
  memory (triggered by the storeroom plaster, returns to it):** the plaster first ("It was thin in places and never floated smooth."; the
  trowel's ridges, the straw, "a patch beside the jamb"); then April 1979, at fourteen, with her mother (no venue named): "In April of 1979
  some of the casts had crossed the Atlantic. My mother took me up on the train to see them on a Saturday. I was fourteen, with a head cold
  and a book report due on the Monday." / "in a long, dim gallery on platforms behind a rope" / the man on his side "with his knees up" /
  "His tunic had rucked at the hip, and the folds of it stood out in the plaster." / "One sandal had pressed its laces into his instep." /
  "a woman in a fur hat told her husband she had thought they would be bigger." / "Further along, a dog lay twisted on its back with its
  legs in the air and its collar still on." / "We stayed until a guard came through ringing a hand bell. In the station my mother bought me
  a pretzel from a cart." / half of it carried "in its paper as far as Trenton". **The carbonized loaf: 0 on the page** (no briquette, and
  no credential paper). Ends on the failed management at the plaster: "Kallia's plaster was cold. I took my fingers from it, and some while
  later they were back on the patch by the jamb." **The laugh (NON-NEGOTIABLE; new shape: aloud, the wrist, no wool, no sink):** triggered
  by Neapolis → Halloran's offer, paraphrased ("He had offered me my father's bed in the house the survey rented, a room with a window in
  it, for the season."; "I had said Greece, perhaps.") → her father's summers ("My father went to the past every summer of my childhood. He
  came home lean and dark, with his boots in a grocery bag and his head still full of somebody else's century. Every year it was going to be
  next year, and then next year there was never a spare bed."; "His last permit had been for Campania. I had signed it over to Halloran in
  April.") → "And the god of Greece had heard my question out, with a gladiator standing for me at the screen, and found me a place in
  Campania after all. He had outranked Halloran. He had picked the town." The laugh: "The laugh was loud, the loudest I had made in that
  house in all my months there. It went straight out past Valens and over the court. A Thessalian stopped snoring. I put my wrist across my
  mouth and it came round the sides anyway, in bits, high, with a squeak in the top of it that I had never heard myself make. My eyes stayed
  dry. The muscles under my ribs hurt. It stopped some while after the funny part had gone out of it." It wakes Valens. **Hand at a
  threshold, unannounced:** "My hand crossed the dark between us. It hung above his shoulder" … "I drew the hand in." **Body rebel
  (unmanaged):** "I held the jar under the spout until it was full. I kept on holding it." The water runs "down my ribs, very cold" until a
  woman with a jug speaks. **The plan (NON-NEGOTIABLE, as reasoning, not a list):** "I made the plan while my feet warmed." (the S8 opener);
  the dead stranger's silver plus Euthydikos's bowl (**the Ch.9/Ch.13 bowl thread is now ACCEPTED in intention**: "It could wait for the god
  to close his doors for the winter, and I could wait with it"); a man ("A husband or a brother; failing those, an owner." / "The one man I
  had was asleep within reach of my hand with a price on him in Corinth." / "I left him out of the plan. He kept being in it."); **the moral
  wrinkle, faced once:** "Whoever took me to that town, the plan left him there." and Felix ("It was Italy. The plan had no boy in it, and I
  kept it so."); the sea in spring ("The sailing season closed with the autumn and opened again in spring. The boats going west at the next
  new moon were for people who had the fare and a man. Next spring, then."; Saint Paul: "His grain ship went aground on Malta, and another
  Alexandrian that had wintered there brought him in to Puteoli with the spring."); Italy (the coast road, the tombs, "a door that was only
  paint", "years ahead of the mountain"); "A year to get there. Seven, after that, to watch a door in."; the invisible-join belief intact
  ("I could join a thing so the join never showed", for Ch.20 to break); "It was a good plan. I said it over once more, bowl to door." (the
  draft's closing judgment is cut). **CH13-7 (the cave):** "That left the cave." (the draft's pointer back to Ch.13 is cut); "I lay and
  argued it both ways." and keeps it: "I wanted it shut. Before I crossed a sea away from that cave I wanted to stand in it in daylight,
  with my fingers on the crack, and feel nothing come through. After that the one door left in the world would be the door the plan ended
  at, and I could stop listening for the other." / "I meant to go up as soon as it was light, with Valens and the mules if he would come,
  and alone if he would rather sleep." The chapter ends toward that errand (Kallia: "He's gone down for the mules," … "You're to wait for
  him at the bottom of the climb."). Credential asides 0 (gate); teeth 0 ("behind my teeth" is the word, not the obsession); laughs 1
  (hers); memories 1 (the casts; the father's summers are a habitual run inside the laugh trigger, Halloran one sentence); "could have" 1

- **DISTINCTNESS GUARANTEE:** the only voice in the book that sees how a thing was made and where it was
  mended, against Valens, who sees where a thing or a man will move next.
- **Sample line:** "Somebody's glued this before, look, there's an old line under the new one, and
  they did it in a hurry with something that's gone yellow; whoever it was loved it more than they knew
  how to fix it." (Calibration only. Its absent-workman verdict, "in a hurry" above all, is SPENT on the page at 6 chapters:
  see at-risk after Ch.9 polish cycle 1.)

#### VALENS ("Lupus") — muleteer, former murmillo, freedman; b. c. AD 38 in Thrace (Ch.6–44)
- **Attention / worldview:** footing, exits, weather, and the moods of animals and men, which are one
  skill to him. He reads bodies before faces and asks who saw. His worldview: whoever is close enough
  to know you is close enough to sell you, so be useful and be nobody.
- **Vocabulary band:** Latin-plain, arena and road register; concrete nouns (ground, rope, hoof, gate,
  bread, ship, weight, rain). **No abstractions in his mouth:** never honor, destiny, justice or fate.
  In Greek (Part One) he is flatly literal, without idiom, using a tool he never learned to enjoy. In
  Latin (Part Two) he is a shade looser and allows himself a dry understatement. His Thracian childhood
  surfaces only as the onion memory, never as words.
- **Syntax fingerprint:** short, level declaratives. He rarely subordinates, and **never uses a simile
  or comparison in speech.** His questions are practical (where, how far, how heavy, who saw). He asks
  *why* only of plans, never of feelings. He understates danger to almost nothing.
- **Rhythm:** statements in ones and twos with silence between. His longest speeches are evidence given
  in strict chronological order (the telling at the stream, Ch.12; the testimony, Ch.22): a witness, not
  a reflective man. **In Ch.34 alone he talks freely and at length.** That is how ease sounds in him.
- **Evasion — never says:** anything about the men he killed; that he is afraid; the word *love* until
  it is said once, plainly, in Ch.43; any grievance about the frame; any request that Mara stay.
- **Physicality / silence:** stillness. When a conversation comes too close he finds maintenance work
  for his hands, and **it is always a different object** (a hoof, a girth strap, a lamp wick, a knife
  edge, the potted fig, a child's sandal strap). It is never a fixed gesture and never ends a scene. He
  is enormously careful with small objects (the watch) and gentle with animals and children. He hands the
  chicken to someone else.
- **Verbal tic:** none — distinguished by attention (footing, bodies, animals) and syntax (short, level,
  unsubordinated, no comparisons)
- **Bleed guard:** must never notice materials or workmanship the way Mara does. When he admires the
  watch, it is as a well-made *working* thing ("It's alive"), not as craft. No wry asides.
- **Chaos on the page (subjects, never phrases):** **figs** (Ch.11, 20, 34; spent after that).
  **Mind-reading threat** (Ch.6, 11, 19, 21, 28, 37: he is wrong in mundane ways). **Onion memory**
  (Ch.12, 22, 23, 32, 41; never explained). **The mime** (Ch.29: he weeps at a farce).
- **DISTINCTNESS GUARANTEE:** the only speaker who never compares one thing to another and never asks why
  about a feeling, against Mara, whose every other sentence qualifies, compares or asks.
- **Sample line:** "He's lame in the off hind. Not your doing. Hold his head and keep talking to him; he
  likes your voice."
- **Ch.6 as written (Writer, 2026-10-19; first live appearance, Delphi and Parnassus, November AD 70. SYNCED to
  the final page 2026-10-19, after dialogue-polish, disruptor, continuity repair and polish cycle 1. Every quote
  below is on the page, except the cut wordings named as cut in the recast notes):**
  **Body (the page's only body facts):** a scar the length of Mara's finger across the back of his **right**
  hand; whoever closed it was in a hurry and set the stitches too far apart, and it healed "in a ladder, every
  rung raised and white" (Mara's repairer's eye, not his). When he leans on the gray's shoulder, "she had to brace
  a foot under his weight" (size), and his scarred hand "was an older hand than the rest of him" (wear; both read by Mara; no face, eyes,
  teeth or beauty word anywhere in the chapter; his ~32 stays unstated). His thumb covers the watch's crown
  entirely; the band will not go halfway round his wrist, so the watch lives in his belt pouch. Later chapters
  give new body facts in fresh words; do not re-quote the ladder or the hands sentence.
  **Entry behavior:** stops his string short of Kallia's gate and walks the iced lane alone, looking at the
  ground; then to Mara, a woman in a gateway with a jar: "Ashes. For the ice. Before they come up." Throws them in
  low, flat fans where a hoof will come down, and brings the mules up singly. Mara learns his name from Kallia
  ("Not in my court, Valens"). He never asks hers ("No."). Sells Kallia split oak ("Split this month. It'll
  burn." / "That was pine. This is oak." / "Five. It's oak."), answers her fast Phocian "a word at a time" and
  gives one coin back; to her questions: "The last ship went out of Kirrha eight days ago." / "Oil. Wine from
  Crete. A man with dogs to sell." / "I don't carry letters." Of Mara's crosswise woodstack: "Who showed you
  that?" (whether a load stands: his axis, not workmanship).
  **Syntax and register as written:** plain, contracted Greek, spoken "slowly and flatly, every ending in its
  place"; Mara follows all of it (and loses the charcoal burner), against her uncontracted book Greek. Short level
  declaratives in ones and twos, almost no subordination. Questions practical only: "Pan's cave. What's up
  there?" / "With what?" / "How long is that?" / "How long does it go?" / "And if a man forgets?" / "And when it's
  tight?" / "Who makes them?" / "For hire?" / "What stops it rocking?" His one *why* is of a plan ("Why tomorrow?
  It'll be there in the spring."). He reckons time and risk in his own consumables: her "until the long hand is at
  the eight" becomes "Most of a pine, then"; "Two more pines after this one"; "I'm paid for the down as well as
  the up." **No comparison or simile in his speech:** dialogue-polish recast "like a book" as "You talk a
  schoolmaster's Greek." and "like the stones in the sanctuary" as "You read the letters on the rock without
  spelling them out."; the writer recast "longer" as "a morning and more". Bleed watch (eval #16): "The widow
  carries her own water again." / "It's his. Whoever goes up in the spring." lean into Kallia's who-pays-the-labor
  axis; from Ch.7 to Ch.12 he reckons in ground, bodies and animals, and Kallia in labor.
  **Attention on the page:** ice and ashes; the stair ("The stair's ice from the second turn"); sacking bound on
  the hooves with the twine pulled tight in his teeth (on the cliff, and fresh at the kiln); the ground under the
  snow read by the lie of the walls and the tops of the thornbushes; the crust tried with his heel before he
  trusts it; the buried ditch; the weather ("There's snow coming over the ridge"; "Snow by afternoon"); a man with
  a donkey on the path below, watched through two turns and then three; her hands ("Those never carried a jar
  till this month."); and what he hears in the dark (her two trips to the spring before they left). Footing
  orders, each caused by terrain on the page: "Hold that. She'll pull." / "Step where she steps. Not where I
  step." / "Sit back. Hold the saddle, not her mouth. Let her find it." / "Ditch." / "Keep to the wall. The edge
  has gone along this bit." Of her English talk on the cliff he catches only "Philadelphia" ("There's a
  Philadelphia in Asia. … Past Sardis. A man I knew came from there." / "Past Asia there's only Parthians.").
  **Mind-reading (wrong in mundane ways):** the donkey man is "Somebody for you" (a charcoal burner); she was
  digging for "Silver. Whatever you ran with."; and the long reading on the last descent, given with his back to
  her: "He'll have you all over the town by the next market. … So here's what I make of you." In order: down off
  the top in the autumn, by herself, in trousers; hands that never worked; the letters on the rock read without
  spelling; "You've got Latin" (his second use; cap reached); a thing on her wrist "no smith in Achaea could
  make"; no name, hers or her people's. "That's a big house. Corinth, or Patrae. Somebody there wants you back, a
  father or a husband, and whoever he is, he's got the money to look. And there's one man in Delphi who'd take a
  mule up Parnassus in the snow." The close is self-exposure, not prediction: the fear underneath is his own
  traceability, and he says nothing about anyone coming. Mara: "Most of it was true, and the whole of it was
  wrong."
  **The watch:** taken as payment the evening before ("Paid now. Not up there. Now."). "He was a long while over
  the back of it, and over the band where the three links had come out"; he watches the seconds hand make one
  circuit with his face unchanged ("Something's going in it." / "Where's the shadow?" / "By itself."). Winds it
  BACKWARD with great care, his thumb covering the crown, stopping to listen to the loose click "as if the click
  were what it was for"; Mara lets him turn it twice more before she can say stop, gives it three turns forward,
  and he finishes until the crown pushes back. "I won't drop it." In the cave he shows her the dial on his palm
  without handing it over (twice) and closes his fingers over it at five to two. Refuses to sell it back ("The
  watch." / "It's a good watch." / "You paid it to go up. You went up." / "You said he didn't need it."). Repeats
  her rule once: "Forward. Till it stops, and not past." (There is no "Not past" exchange; dialogue-polish cut the
  three-line antiphon.)
  **The coin:** pinches her 50-drachma piece off her palm (brass, an old bearded head; Mara: "A poet.") and returns
  it: "That buys nothing up there. Or down here."
  **Mules:** unnamed (the gray, the brown that ate Kallia's pomegranate last winter, a dun): "A name's for calling.
  They come when they see the barley bag." Talks to them low in Latin (*Ambula, asini filia*): "They came from
  Corinth. They don't know any Greek." … "These know Latin."
  **Pan and the cave:** leaves a corner of crust on the ledge ("It's his house." / "I pay him." / "It's what I
  do."); asks what the inscription says and answers "That's what the goat boys say it says" (he does not admit to
  reading). Makes fire slowly with steel, flint and bracket fungus ("No."). Holds each pine off to one side so
  that his shadow falls away from her work; lights the next from the stub and grinds the stub out under his boot.
  **The laugh and the bread:** once, at her scream: "short and surprised, and ran on briefly after, a laugh with
  nobody at the end of it" (cycle 1 cut the sentence comparing it to the Sacred Way laugh). "Good." The bread from
  the front of his tunic, broken across his knee, warm. "He finished what was in his mouth first." / "It was the
  first thing you did up here I understood." / "A thief doesn't fill the widow's jar before she goes."
  **Looking after her without saying so (the descent):** "Where do you sleep?" / "Dry?" / "Does she hit you?" /
  "She feeds you." / "Stay where you are. She'll keep you while you work."
  **Evasions on the page:** where he's from ("North."); where he learned Latin ("On the road."; Mara: "Latin is
  not learned on this road."); her name (never asked); her story ("Don't.", then "Keep to the wall…", the refusal
  done as footing). Would have walked past the kiln; the gray turned for the warmth. Lies smoothly for her there
  ("She's the widow Kallia's. Down by the spring." / "Since the olives." / "She came off a ship." / "She hit
  Pan.").
  **Hands:** all his maintenance business in Ch.6 is mule tack (girths, a lead tied into a tail, sacking,
  unlashing). Vary the object class from Ch.7 (audit N-16: a knife edge, a lamp wick, a sandal strap).
  **Last line:** gives her the duffel, then the shovel: "Men will come asking. You went up by yourself. You never
  had a watch." The threat is spoken once, here, as the pull.
  **Spent in Ch.6 (do not reuse):** the backward winding (every later winding is forward and worded fresh);
  "Something's going in it"; "Where's the shadow?"; "It's his house" / "I pay him"; "A name's for calling";
  "These know Latin"; "He's cheap"; "Good." after the scream; "a laugh with nobody at the end of it"; "It was the
  first thing you did up here I understood."; "Most of a pine, then"; "So here's what I make of you." and the
  evidence-in-order reading; "one man in Delphi who'd take a mule up Parnassus in the snow"; "Keep to the wall.
  The edge has gone along this bit."; "You've got Latin" (2, cap reached); "Men will come asking" (once, the
  pull); "You never had a watch"; "Forward. Till it stops, and not past."; "his hands were older than the rest of
  him"; the scar "in a ladder".

- **Ch.7 as written (Writer, 2026-10-19; brief, the middle of her month of public silence. SYNCED to the final page
  2026-10-19, after dialogue-polish, disruptor, continuity repair, polish cycle 1 and the three finalize fixes; every
  quote below is on the page, checked by script):** comes to
  Kallia's gate with the mules for the fifth coin. Mara, in her month of public silence and taught to look at men's
  feet, sees ONLY his boots (rawhide, laced to the shin, one lace broken and knotted). Lines: "It burned." / "Oak
  doesn't spit." / to Mara: "She's taught you to walk," / "You've stopped talking." / to Kallia's "She's simple": "No."
  Leaves without the coin (Kallia: "Once on my good hearthstone and once in the lentils."). No hands, no face, no
  romance signal; Mara wonders only whether he wound the watch before he saw to the mules or after, "and whether he
  remembered which way". His boat lie to the burner has become Mara's legal story (Kallia adds Syracuse). He still
  does not know her name. Spent: the boots-only sighting; "No." as his whole answer to a claim about her; the
  spitting-oak exchange (see at-risk after Ch.7: the fifth coin is settled off the page).

- **Ch.9 as written (Writer, 2026-10-19; the eval's escalation met: one spoken exchange, practical, before the notice.

  every quote below is on the page, checked by script):** "At the back gate Valens was unloading split wood off a line of mules
  into the priest's woodshed." The gray is tied apart with her load off, a pot of pitch hung on the gatepost; he works it into a
  saddle gall on her withers. "Her ears were flat. When she swung her head round to bite him he put her nose away with his elbow
  and went on working." (the disruptor cut the two-fingered caress as a romance cue: do not restore it). Lines: "For her back.
  The new saddle's rubbed her." (one speech) / "What for?" / "That's the god's," / "Will it stand?" / "Somebody will." / "An
  obol." / "He'll pay." (dialogue-polish cut "Warm it first…" as his spent order-plus-consequence shape and a bleed into Mara's
  materials axis.) Attention: before answering, "His eyes left me for the court: to the stand on its sack in the sun, and to the
  old man on his basket by the hives, who had gone to sleep sitting up." (who is watching). "He wiped his fingers on the gray's
  mane, unhooked the pot and broke the cake of pitch in it with the haft of his knife"; the pitch is "almost black, cold and
  brittle, with a mule hair in it." "He bent to her withers again." (the "[S] bent to [the X] again" frame is CLOSED: see at-risk
  after Ch.9). Still does not know her name; no romance signal; no closeness, so no practical-order deflection. **The notice (no
  words on either side):** he comes into the square at its upper corner "with the gray behind him on a slack rope. She carried
  nothing."; "at his ordinary pace"; "He halted at the board."; stands "while a slow man might read it once, and then as long
  again" (the page does not confirm that he reads; Ch.11 keeps that reveal); "He turned his head and found me there by the
  column."; "He looked back at me while a cart came in at the top of the square and rolled down past us"; takes the gray "down
  the square at a walk, past the men still at the eel-seller, to the trough below the fountain"; last line: "He let the gray
  drink first." Nobody in Delphi calls him Lupus; the men at the board joke about the name without connecting it to him (the
  Hostile reader's open question for Ch.10–11: a scarred Thracian at the board in daylight, unconnected by five men). His
  "How long…?" stays at 3 book-wide (all Ch.6). Spent: the pitch exchange; "He'll pay."; "Somebody will."; the silent look at
  the board; letting the gray drink first; the bite elbowed away.

  before he's had his bread he does something to it, with his back to me".) **The breeching:** "It's been breaking all winter." / "I

  that." / "A fig that gives has gone to the wasps." / "You pick when the skin splits by itself. A day in the shade first, on the
  tree's own leaves. After that the sun, whole, on a rush, turned every morning, and in at nightfall, ahead of the dew." / "Where's

- **Ch.12 as written (Writer, 2026-10-20; "The Kirrha Road". SYNCED to the FINAL page 2026-10-20 (polish cycle 1 + F1–F2; continuity
  W-21, W-22): every quote below is on the page, checked by script):** **The fight, fast and unglamorous, mostly unseen:** before it,
  to the stranger, "You want somebody else." and to Mara, "Go on up to the boy,"; "The hand stayed at Valens's side."; "I saw Valens's
  hand shut on the wrist above it."; the stick "came down across Valens's shoulders"; he and the talker "pitched off the edge of the
  road together, into the ditch below the terrace"; "Something struck stone down there, twice."; "Valens climbed out with the stick
  coming down at him. It landed on his forearm, and he kept coming, and the big man dropped it and ran."; "There was a knife in his
  hand, and it was not his own." **His first use of her name is a warning, untagged, from the ditch: "Mara."** (never a callback; do
  not stage a second "first time"). **Practical after:** "Are you cut?" / "He looked for blood on me regardless."; he cuts the dead
  man's purse ("When Valens climbed back up he had a purse on a cut thong. He had left the man everything else."); "Get the string on
  to the ford," (to Felix); "This is the god's road." … "Let the god's people find him." / "That's what it was. A traveler with his
  purse cut." (he authors the finders' story); the cracked jar: he cut the ropes "and the jar came off into his arms, and he heaved it
  over the terrace edge, down among the olives", then "picked a sliver of pot out of the hair there and threw it after the man"; he
  reads the walkers far below and moves the string ("Now,"); at the ford, "Valens chose a stone from the bank, near a jar's weight, and
  roped it opposite the single one on the mule that had lost hers." (no knot, no hands read as a mood).
- **Voice under pressure (first shown on the page, Ch.12):** his speech does not change register under pressure. In the fight he says
  one word, "Mara.", untagged, from the ditch. Straight after it his lines are as short and practical as on any working day ("Are you
  cut?" / "Get the string on to the ford," / "Now,"). The pressure goes into his body instead, and only once the work is done, at the
  fall: "They were shaking." / "It started in his shoulders and ran out along his arms, and the water jumped off his fingers with it."
  / "the thumb kept slipping off the nail" / "He gave that up and held his hands still under the water, and they would not be still." /
  "He was breathing through his mouth." He turns his shoulder to hide the hands, "and I saw them anyway". **Mara steadies him (the
  emotional surprise: his, not hers):** she takes his wrists and presses them flat to the stone under the fall, and does not speak; "It
  stopped by degrees." / "He let his forehead come to rest on my hair. He stayed there." / "His stayed flat on the stone. He took them
  down himself." He says nothing during it and nothing about it afterward. His next line is about the camera ("Is it broken?"), and the
  one after that begins the telling, in the same flat witness's Greek as his road talk. Under pressure he gets plainer, never louder
  and never figurative. **The shaking is a one-time body rebel, SPENT as an event:** no later chapter gives him trembling hands as his
  pressure sign (that would be a signature gesture; Ch.23's "he is the one comforted" finds another body and sense). **Injuries carried
  into Ch.13 (three days on; do not show him unmarked):** "There was a graze along his cheekbone with grit in it." (he "plainly knew
  nothing about it"); "Across the left one a welt stood up where the stick had caught it, red and raised, and the knuckles of that hand
  were split."; the stick across the shoulders.
- **Ch.12, the rest of the page:** **Onion memory (first surfacing), triggered:** Mara treads on a clump of flat leaves at the pool
  ("they smelled green and sharp under my heel"): "Onions," … "They hung them under the eaves. In strings, to dry." Never explained.
  **Trust acts:** "He untied the pouch from his belt and put it into my hands. Through the leather the watch was going."; returned at
  the ford: "At the stepping stones I gave him back his pouch. He put the purse in my hand." (no look inside, no line). He washes the
  stranger's knife after the closeness ("running his thumb along the groove to clear it"). **The camera:** at the stream he asks only
  "Is it broken?" (a working thing, his axis). **The telling (his longest speech so far; a witness's chronology, no grievance):** "They
  gave me the wooden sword at Corinth," / "He had the year right, the one on the road." / "The Gaul was older than me, and slow on his
  left side. Nobody on the benches knew that. The emperor never got out of his chair." / "The lanista had a copy made for me on a
  tablet. I kept it in a box." / "After the sword I needed work. Celer was legate then, on the proconsul's staff." / "I swore to her in
  his atrium, in front of his household gods." / "When he was away I slept across her door. Nobody laid a hand on her in two years. I
  was a good bodyguard. That was the use of me." / "He'd bought a house he couldn't pay for, out where the cypresses are." / "He was
  sorry about it. He said so, more than once, with her standing there. She didn't look at me." / "One was the girl who did her hair,
  and she cried all through it. They'd been shown where to stand." / "Put a wife away for that, and you keep a share of what she
  brought. Her father had given plenty with her." / "by the fishpond, where the house could watch" / "his steward went through my box
  and took the tablet" / "The magistrate's book is still in the record office at Corinth, with my name in it." / "I was over his garden
  wall the same night. A fisherman took me across the gulf for my belt buckle." / "They asked my name and I gave them Valens." / the
  mules "off a Corinthian dealer who couldn't feed them till spring" / "Her brother took her home," … "Celer kept the boy."
- **The legal claim as the page now has it (continuity W-21; the prose wins over foundation §3):** the freedom WAS registered: "I put
  the Gaul down, and he sent the sword out to me across the sand, and a magistrate wrote my name in his book." The registration is not
  in dispute. Celer's claim is a prior sale: "His story was that he'd bought me from the lanista the month before the games, so the
  lanista had no right to free me, and the magistrate had written down a mistake. So I was his." In plot terms Celer's case rests on a
  backdated bill of sale. The words "bill of sale" and "backdated" are NOT on the page; Valens says only "His story was". **Consequence
  for Ch.15, 17 and 22:** the Corinth record proves the lanista freed him before witnesses and the emperor, but on its own it no longer
  refutes Celer. The Rome suit has to defeat the sale itself: the lanista's books, the date, or the fact that Celer hired him for wages
  after the games ("After the sword I needed work. Celer" … "wanted a man to walk behind his wife"). Valens never argues the law; he
  gives the chronology and stops. (Foundation §3's "never registered before a magistrate" and the Ch.17/Ch.22 outline beats still carry
  the old claim: flagged for the architect/orchestrator, not patched in this file.)
- **Ch.12, knowledge path, Felix, plans:** **Knowledge path (one passage):** "Last autumn a freedman of her brother's found me at the
  harbor," … "The brother's gone to law for her dowry. He wanted me to come and stand up and tell the court it was a lie. I said I
  wouldn't. He'll have told them where he found me. Inside the month that notice went up in Corinth. You saw the month on it." / "I saw
  it too." (it stands alone) / "Five hundred for me walking," … "Whoever wants five hundred brings a rope. Those three brought none. He
  doesn't want me back. He wants me in a ditch." He gives NO reason for refusing the freedman (evasion: fear). How Celer knew the
  harbor is Valens's own inference ("He'll have told them where he found me."). **With Felix:** the crust lie is Felix's own ("It was
  that crust," … "It was off."); Valens answers "Don't give the goat any more of it," and gives the cover story ("If anybody in the
  town asks, we came on a man in the ditch below the ford, and the road had been at him before us.") / "You came on a man in a ditch."
  / "Tell it anyway." / to "He believed me": "He'd settled on robbers back on the road," / to "Did he go down straight off?": "No."
  (his ONE bare "No." in Ch.12; the Ch.11 lie answered truly, without "one blow"). **With the graybeard:** "There's thorn all along
  this road," **Plans, stated, no request:** "Three days," / "A knife in the precinct would have a hundred men on it. They'll want me
  on a road, not in a crowd." / "The goat comes to the yard the evening before." / "When it's over, the mules are for the high
  grazing," … "Above the cave. Up there you see a man coming a whole morning off. I'll stay up the summer." (he does not ask her to
  come) / Kallia: "She hears what the donkey man heard." … "Kallia, then. Nobody else." **Names for him on the page:** the stranger's
  "Lupus" and "*Rudiarius*". Nobody calls him "mule-man" in Ch.12 (the word is in Ch.6 and Ch.11 only); the graybeard says "You've been
  in the thorns." **Page counts (FINAL):** no simile in his speech; bare "No." 1; bare "Felix." 0; walk-offs after a line 2 ("Now," and
  the slap on the hindmost mule; "He was first down the goat path to the ford."); narrated non-answers 0; stopped-work tell 0; a knot
  near his hands 0; boot lace 0 (cut, continuity W-05). **Spent in Ch.12:** the shaking hands held still at the fall; forehead on her
  hair; the pouch handed over to hold; "Is it broken?"; the purse cut to stage a robbery; "Let the god's people find him."; "The
  emperor never got out of his chair."; "That was the use of me." (the at-risk single use); "Whoever wants five hundred brings a
  rope."; "He wants me in a ditch."; "There's thorn all along this road,"; "He'd settled on robbers back on the road,"; "Tell it
  anyway."; the slept-across-her-door detail (said once; Ch.14's doorway is staged without pointing at it); a stone roped to balance a
  load.
